  - **⛔⛔ `ReapableShareAt` IS DIMENSIONALLY WRONG, AND THAT IS THE BUG.** It scales a farm's field by `budgeted ÷ haul` — and those are **not the same kind of quantity**: `budgeted` is a *round trip inside the field* (4 ticks), `haul` is a *one-way walk to a store* (10). **The ratio is not a share of anything**, and that it lands near the right answer at ten ticks is arithmetic coincidence.
  - **⭐⭐ THE MEASUREMENT, one hand, ten years, shipped config, committed ground posed at each level so the curve is visible:**

    | farm → store | the cap sowed | what it can actually bring in | autumn spent **idle** at the cap |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 10 ticks | 5 | **6** | **27%** |
    | 16 ticks | 3 | **5** | **45%** |
    | 22 ticks | 2 | **4** | **55%** |

    **The cap cut the field, the farmer then had nothing left to do, and the idleness read back as proof the field had been too big.** Four sessions hunted a physical cause that was not there.
  - **⛔⛔ AND THIRTEEN TILES TEN TICKS OUT IS PHYSICALLY IMPOSSIBLE — which is the honest answer to Joe's complaint.** *"A farmer plants 5 tiles of the 13."* Autumn is **120 ticks**; thirteen tiles at that distance needs about **230**. **The farm was short of one or two tiles, not eight.** The lever that buys thirteen is **the walk** — the same farmer beside a granary commits the whole field, and measures out at 21 before the ground runs out. *Told to Joe before anything was built, because the fix he was promised was not available.*
  - **⭐ THE FIX IS MEMORY, NOT A BETTER FORMULA — and "no formula fits" is a finding rather than a shrug.** The true ceiling depends on the market's drain rate, the painted ground's shape, how full the granary is and how many hands turned up; the measured curve fits **no closed form** (`season ÷ (reap + walk)` wants a different constant at every distance, moving the wrong way with distance). **So a farm sows what it has already brought in** — a high-water mark, per hand, clamped to `FieldTilesOneFarmerKeeps` and re-reckoned when the walk changes. **It converges on exactly the ledger's numbers — 6, 5 and 4 — without ever being told them**, which is the strongest evidence the mechanism is right.
  - **⭐⭐ THE RED CHECK DELETED A FEATURE, AND THIS IS THE ENTRY'S MOST TRANSFERABLE PART.** Two drafts had the farm commit `learned + 1` a year and latch once a tile rotted. **Breaking the probe turned NOTHING red** — settled memory and tiles reaped identical at all three distances (6/5/4 learned, 72/60/48 reaped). The reason: `HarvestOneFarmCanBringIn` multiplies by the hands standing in the field *at that moment*, so **a farm with two hands in spring and one by autumn already commits ground for two.** *D86's live-allowance rule was always going to over-reach; the memory only has to notice.* **A probe on top would have been the fifth invisible no-op this project has rejected** (D56, D177, D187) — and the failure modes agreed: with no probe the worst case is today's behaviour, with one it is a tile rotting every year. **14 reds across 7 breaks, and the break that scored zero is the one that changed the design.**
  - **⛔ `farm_store_cap` IS DEAD FOR THE SECOND TIME, BY A DIFFERENT ROUTE.** An **8.7× buffer** (871 against 100 — thirteen armfuls instead of one and a half) moved the ceiling from **6 tiles to 6** at ten ticks and **5 to 5** at sixteen. The haul ledger says why: even with that room it took **23 of 72 loads**, because it fills once and the market cannot keep it drained. *Two independent measurements; stop proposing it.*
  - **⭐ AND THE PLAYER IS TOLD, WHILE THEY CAN STILL MOVE IT (§4.3).** Nothing in the game said a farm far from a store halves its own harvest — the existing placement warning measures the walk to the *village*, which is the wrong walk for a farm. Marking a farmhouse now names the haul, the tiles it will actually keep, and the fix. **Warned, never refused** (D43, D86). The farm's own panel stopped claiming *"every hand here can keep 13"* on a farm that can keep six — **a number the building cannot achieve is worse than no number.**
  - ⚠️ **A doc bug found on the way in, recorded rather than quietly patched:** §6 said *"See D186"* and **there is no D186** — nor a D184. The farm's parking was never written up as a decision at all, so the largest known-open thing in the game lived only in the handoff. *This entry is where it lives now.*
  - **689 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 691.** **One golden moved — the seam**, the only village in the suite that plants a farmhouse; **both fifty-year goldens are unmoved**, which is the check that matters, because a farm's memory reaching a village with no farm in it would be a bug rather than a re-base.

- **D193 · 2026-08-23 · ⚠️ "PHASE 3 IS COMPLETE" WAS WRONG, AND I WROTE IT.** D190's title claimed the phase was done because its three landings were. **§11's Definition of Done has item 7 outstanding — the at-risk line — and apprenticeship (§5) is unbuilt**, which is the pillar's actual point (*"that skill dies with the person unless an elder apprentices a youth"*) and the thing §10's anti-vacuity guard is written for and **cannot be written without**.
  - **⛔ THREE LANDINGS IS NOT A PHASE.** The landings are a *sequencing* device — they exist so a regression is attributable (D157) — and finishing them says nothing about the Definition of Done. **Two different lists, and only one of them is the gate.**
  - **⚠️ THE COST OF LEAVING IT WOULD HAVE BEEN A SESSION.** D159 found five specs claiming *"not started"* for systems that had shipped; this is the same drift running the other way — a document claiming **done** for work that has not begun, read at exactly the moment a new session is orienting. **The handoff and `skills-catalog.md`'s status line both said it too**, so it would have been believed three times over.
  - **Corrected in place rather than silently**, per the log's own convention: D190's title is struck through and annotated. *A decisions log that quietly rewrites its own claims is worth less than one that shows where it was wrong.*
- **D192 · 2026-08-23 · ⭐ A FIRE THAT ACTUALLY WARMS YOU — AND A DERIVATION THAT AVOIDED CHOOSING A NUMBER STILL CHOSE ONE.** Joe, after playing: *"recovering from the brink at a fire should take 5 days, not 15. Fire warm up should be much faster."*
  - **⛔ THE OLD RATE WAS DERIVED, AND THAT IS EXACTLY HOW IT ESCAPED NOTICE.** `ThawPerTickAtAFire` mirrored `ExposurePerTickOutdoors` on the stated grounds that mirroring *"needs no number of its own"*. True — and it quietly chose **fifteen days to thaw**, half a winter, for two phases. **Check what a derivation came out as, not merely that it is principled.**
  - **Now the third line of D45's table, stated plainly:** fifteen days outdoors kills, twenty-five under a fireless roof kills, **five at a fire brings you all the way back**. Bounded below by a measurement rather than taste — a fire that *zeroed* the count was built and rejected, because villagers spend **76% of winter at a lit hearth** and nobody froze in 120 years.
  - **⭐⭐ IT EXPOSED A REAL BUG WITH NOTHING TO DO WITH WARMTH.** A marketer who gets hungry mid-delivery eats the load out of their own arms (D10 — *nobody starves holding dinner*) and then **walked the rest of the leg carrying nothing**. `AMarketerNeverWalksAnEmptyLeg` permitted it on the tick they ate, which is one tick of a walk that takes many. **The bug was always there; a faster thaw merely moved the timings until it happened at year 90 instead of never in the run under test.** They re-decide now.
  - **⭐ AND A GUARD WAS REFRAMED RATHER THAN RELAXED, AFTER I CAUGHT MYSELF DOING THE LATTER.** `AFirewoodLimitStopsTheWoodcutters` allowed one batch of overshoot — a *guess* at how much work can be in flight. My first fix was `seats × split`, a second guess, and it did not even cover the observed number. **Two guesses at a tolerance is the point at which the tolerance is the wrong thing to assert.** It now checks what the limit actually promises: production stopped and stayed stopped, the cap bit hard, and **nobody froze for it**. ⚠️ Worth knowing: **the sheds drain to nothing under a limit** — households hold their own fuel — so a guard watching only the shed would read that as a catastrophe. Thirty people, forty years, nobody cold.
- **D191 · 2026-08-23 · ⭐ ELDERS EAT A DEPENDANT'S SHARE, AND THE OLD RULE WAS NEVER A RULE.** Joe asked whether children and elders eat less than adults, then ruled: *"elders should eat the same volume of food as children."*
  - **⛔ THE ANSWER WAS "CHILDREN YES, ELDERS NO", AND NOBODY HAD DECIDED THAT.** `MealCostFor` had exactly one branch and it tested for `LifeStage.Child`, so **an elder ate a full adult portion while producing at `vigour_min_percent`** — because the other arm of an `if` caught them, not because ageing had been ruled on. *A number nobody picked is still a number the game is balanced on*, and it took a question from the chair to surface it.
  - **⭐ `child_food_share_percent` IS RENAMED `dependant_food_share_percent` IN THE SAME COMMIT.** A key that governs elders while calling itself *child* is precisely the name-that-lies this project keeps catching (D148, D188), and *dependant* is the word the economy already uses — `RequiredDependants`. **The shipped file never set it**, so the rename costs nothing and the default of 50 is unchanged.
  - **⚠️ It makes the economy easier, which is safe by construction:** `VillageEconomy` derives a survival **floor**, and a village whose old people eat half needs less than the floor demands. Nothing derived had to move.
  - **⭐ The build menu says *Forager* now**, the last place still saying *Gatherer* after D188 — the same one-job-two-names bug surfacing in a third panel, and the reason a rename has to be finished rather than mostly done.
  - **AND THE HEARTH QUESTION, ANSWERED FROM THE CODE:** *does a house consuming firewood warm villagers faster than one without?* **It is a sign flip rather than a rate change.** At a fire, cold goes **down** 100 a tick; under a fireless roof it still goes **up**, at 60; outdoors, up at 100. Threshold 6,000. **⚠️ And it is *having* firewood, not burning it** — `ShelterAt` returns `Fire` on `Stockpile.Firewood > 0`, so a household with one log is exactly as warm as one with a hundred, right up until the log runs out.
  - **675 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 677**; four goldens re-taken, including the posed no-op guard whose *reference* village moved for a reason that has nothing to do with skill.
- **D190 · 2026-08-23 · ⭐⭐ ~~PHASE 3 IS COMPLETE~~ PHASE 3's THREE LANDINGS ARE BUILT, AND D28 IS DISCHARGED AFTER FOUR WEEKS OPEN.** ⛔ **The "complete" in this title was wrong when it was written and is corrected here rather than quietly edited** (D193): §11's Definition of Done has **item 7 outstanding** — the at-risk line — and **apprenticeship (§5) is unbuilt**, which is the pillar's actual point and the thing §10's anti-vacuity guard is written for. *Three landings is not a phase*, and a future session reading "complete" would have skipped both. Landing 3: the founders arrive as **a master, a journeyman and two novices with seeded trades** (§3.2c), and every villager is drawn **a personal rhythm at birth** (§3.5). Joe's calls on both: *"1 master, 1 journeyman, 2 novices"* and *"novice / apprentice / journeyman / master"*.
  - **⭐ FIXED SHAPE, SEEDED TRADES — variety in what you can do, never in whether you can live.** Measured across six seeds: **Master woodcutting + Journeyman forestry** (12345), **Master foraging + Journeyman forestry** (2), **Master forestry + Journeyman farming** (42), **Master trading + Journeyman forestry** (11). Same party strength every time, a different speciality every time. A fully seeded roll would make a four-novice seed and a two-master seed *a bad run and a good one rather than two playthroughs*, and §0.1 is that the challenge is in the planning, never in the punishment.
  - **⭐ AND THE UNMEASURED ARM §11 NAMED IS ANSWERED: a master gatherer does NOT make the opening trivial — it changes nothing at all.** Food at the first winter is **identical** across three seeds with one master forager, two, or none, because **nobody forages during the opening**. A master forester moves it 13% and 9%; population at years 1 and 5 is unchanged in every arm.
  - **⛔⛔ THE FIRST VERSION OF THE RHYTHM DID ALMOST NOTHING, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS THE ENTIRE VALUE OF THIS ENTRY.** A stagger that delays somebody's first *step* moved the lockstep from 91% to 88% and left **identical hunger at 100%** — because **hunger is a pure function of ticks since the last meal, so two people who eat on the same tick stay in step for ever however differently they walk.** Identical hunger is one of the two numbers D28 named, and nothing that offsets only movement can touch it. **The rhythm now sets their starting hunger apart as well as their first step.**
  - **⛔⛔ AND THEN BOTH FOUNDING PAIRS DREW THE *SAME* RHYTHM — A STRIDE ARTIFACT, NOT A BROKEN RNG.** Agnes and Wendell both drew 1; Hattie and Otto both drew 2. **Forty raw `NextInt(0, 4)` draws come out 9/11/8/12 — the generator is sound.** But the founding takes four small-range draws *at a fixed stride at the very start of the stream*, and at that stride the first four are **1, 1, 2, 2**. *A generator can be sound and still be the wrong tool for four draws that must differ from each other.* The draw supplies the seeded part; **a rotation by position in the household supplies the guarantee.**
  - **⭐⭐ MEASURED, FIRST FIVE YEARS: identical hunger 100% → 0%, same tile 91% → 80%.** D28 has been open since 2026-07-26 and asked for the fix to work *at the opening* rather than across a century; it does. **The red check is built into the guard** — the same measurement with both switched off must still show the lockstep, or the comparison proves nothing.
  - **⚠️ THREE GUARDS WENT RED THAT §10 HAD PREDICTED WOULD, WITH INSTRUCTIONS.** *"A guard that tries to assert this about the real opening will fail, and the temptation will be to weaken it instead of to pose it properly."* `ANoviceWorksAtExactlyTodaysSpeed` reached for founder #0, who is now a **master**; the fifty-year no-op guard needed **the mixed founding and the rhythm switched off** as well as the speed bonus. **Both posed, neither relaxed** — and `seeded_rhythm` exists as a switch *because the test plan required one*, skipping the draw rather than zeroing it so the old stream reproduces byte for byte.
  - **675 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 677**; three state-hash goldens re-taken in the same commit. **Phase 3's three landings are done. Apprenticeship (§5) and the at-risk line (§7) remain unbuilt.**
- **D189 · 2026-08-23 · ⭐ EAT LESS, BURN MORE — AND THE FARMING COMPLAINT TURNED OUT TO BE THE PARKED FARM BUG WEARING A DIFFERENT HAT.** Joe, after playing: *"lower food consumption -20%, increase firewood consumption +20%"*, and *"gathering brings in more food than farming."*
  - **⭐ FOOD IS EXACTLY −20% AND IT IS A REAL LOOSENING RATHER THAN A RENAME.** `food_per_meal` 5 → 4. `RequiredGatherYield` falls with it, and **`gather_yield` is guarded as a FLOOR rather than an equality** — so leaving the yields where they are hands the village the whole 20% as headroom above the survival line. *Re-deriving the yields down too would have been the same game with smaller numbers*, which is the trap a "consumption" dial sets when everything downstream is derived from it.
  - **⚠️ It is a much bigger lever than it sounds.** Measured at a century on the shipped seed: **population 29 → 40 and food stored 4,631 → 6,980**, with starvation deaths falling 11 → 6.
  - **⚠️ FIREWOOD IS +25%, NOT +20%, AND THE GRID IS WHY.** A winter costs `ceil(days_per_season / interval) × firewood_per_winter_day`, so at one log a burn the only reachable numbers over a thirty-day winter are **8, 10 and 15**. Ten is the closest. Exactly +20% is reachable only as *three logs every ten days*, which hits the number and **changes the rhythm** — a household would need three in hand at once, so a thin larder fails in bursts rather than gradually. Closer-in-spirit won; the burst option is written into the config beside it.
  - **⛔ AND IT DOES NOT MAKE WINTER DANGEROUS, WHICH IS WORTH KNOWING BEFORE ANYBODY ASKS IT TO.** Measured across all four combinations of the two changes: **zero cold deaths in every one.** What +25% buys is a thinner woodpile — firewood held at a century falls **941 → 354** — not a village that freezes. If winter is meant to bite, this dial is not the one that does it.
  - **⛔⛔ AND THE FARMING COMPLAINT IS NOT A YIELD PROBLEM — IT IS `ReapableShareAt`, THE THING WE PARKED.** Joe proposed raising what a crop is worth. Measured per worker-year: **a well-sited farm produces 1,376 food against a forager's 1,076** — farming already wins, and the derivation's *a farmer's year is worth a gatherer's year* target is met. **A farm ten ticks from a store produces 523**, because its field is cut to 40%. *Raising `crop_yield_per_tile` would inflate a derived number to compensate for a bug, and would leave well-sited farms at two and a half times gathering.* Left alone deliberately.
  - **⚠️ THE FIRST INSTRUMENT SAID 357 AGAINST 1,749 AND WAS WRONG.** It counted food into the farm's own store, and a reaper hauling to the granary never touches it. **Counting reaps instead flipped the answer from "gathering wins 5×" to "farming wins by 28%"** — and the wrong number would have justified changing a locked, derived value. *The instrument is as likely to be wrong as the code*, for the second time this session.
  - **Only the two SHIPPED goldens moved**; the fixture pair is untouched, because `VillageFixtures.Village` derives its own economy and never reads these two keys. **667 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 669.**
- **D188 · 2026-08-23 · ⭐ ONE PLACE NAMES A JOB, AND THERE USED TO BE TWO THAT DISAGREED.** `Main.ProfessionName` said **Gatherer** and **Vendor**; `Main.TradeOf` said **forager** and **marketer** — the professions panel and stock rows using one vocabulary, the roster beside every villager's name using the other. **The same job, two words, two panels**, which is D148's finding arriving in the UI.
  - **It stopped being cosmetic when landing 1's skill line landed.** Joe: *"instead of the 'in the fields, in the woods' lines, can it be 'X years as a <profession>'."* That sentence sits **directly under** the roster entry naming the villager, so a third place had to agree with the other two — and two of the three did not. **A phrase that names a trade forces the vocabulary question that scene-setting had let everyone avoid.**
  - **Joe's call: *"forager and marketer win."*** `TradeOf` now lower-cases `ProfessionName` instead of keeping its own list, so exactly one place names a job.
  - ⚠️ **Also fixed a doc that had gone stale rather than wrong-in-passing:** `SkillRow.MasteryYears` still described per-skill mastery numbers as set and derived, which **D182's measurement withdrew** — no row sets one. The column is inert and now says so, in the same standing as `Recordable`.
- **D187 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ MASTERY BITES — PHASE 3 LANDING 2, AND THE MOMENT THE SKILL PILLAR STOPPED BEING BOOKKEEPING.** Skill scales **how long a job takes**, not what it yields (§3.3) — duration first, for a legibility reason rather than an arithmetic one: **a villager who is out longer is visible on the map, and one who brings back less is only visible in a panel.** `SimWorld.WorkTicksFor` is the one door, and the six action durations in `BehaviorSystem` go through it.
  - **⛔ A NOVICE TAKES EXACTLY TODAY'S TICKS, WHICH IS WHAT KEEPS THE ECONOMY STANDING** (§3.2). `VillageEconomy` derives the **survival floor** about the least skilled person in the valley, so a floor that moved by one tick would make every derived number describe a village that no longer exists. **Nobody is ever worse than today; a master is simply better** — and `ANoviceWorksAtExactlyTodaysSpeed` asserts it at every trade.
  - **⛔⛔ AND THE MEASUREMENT FOUND THAT MOST OF THE PLAUSIBLE WIDTHS ARE LITERALLY NO-OPS.** The durations are **3 and 4 ticks**, so the bonus buys nothing until it rounds to a whole tick. **At 17% not one duration moves. At 25% only the four-tick trades do — and a village at 25% produced population and food *identical* to one with the feature switched off.** A setting that reads as *"a quarter faster"* and changes nothing is the invisible no-op this project has rejected four times, and it would have shipped without a probe.
  - **⭐⭐ FIFTY IS MEASURED, AND IT HAS A CLEAN STATEMENT: a master's action takes half the ticks, rounded up.** 3 → 2, 4 → 2. Across three seeds at a century, against the same villages with the bonus at zero: **population 23 → 29, 63 → 65, 15 → 20**, and food stored 3,677 → 4,631 and 4,870 → 9,648. **Population rises on every seed** — D161's mid-game answer arriving. 34% was tried first and is marginal: population unchanged on two seeds of three.
  - **⚠️ THE SPEC PREDICTED A DIFFERENT SHAPE, AND THE MEASUREMENT SAYS OTHERWISE.** §3.2 expected mastery to cash out as *"the same output from fewer hands — and the hands it frees become laborers."* **Laborer counts did not move at all**; the village grew instead. Both are D161's answer, arriving through a different door than the one predicted.
  - **⚠️ IT IS A STEP, NOT A RAMP, AND THAT IS THE TIER MODEL ARRIVING THROUGH ARITHMETIC.** Measured across progress: **3 ticks up to 60% of mastery, 2 from 70% on.** A three-tick action cannot express anything in between, so **the sim already behaves as though there are two tiers because it cannot express any others at these durations** — worth knowing before §12 chooses tier names.
  - **⭐ THE ANTI-VACUITY GUARD LANDING 1 SET UP HAS FIRED, IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION.** `ComputeIgnoringSkills` was byte-identical to the pre-skill goldens through landing 1 and **has now moved**, which is exactly what its own note said must happen when mastery bit. And `FiftyYearsOfVillageAndOnlyTheCountersMoved` went red — **the guard working rather than breaking** — so it is **re-based by posing the village the claim is about** (the bonus at zero) rather than by relaxing what it asserts, which is §10's instruction written in advance and D150's rule.
  - **667 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 669. Three guards checked red**, including the one that catches a width rounding away to nothing.
- **D185 · 2026-08-22 · ⛔⛔ THE VILLAGE NEVER STAFFED A MARKETER BECAUSE A FARM NEEDED EMPTYING — THE BEHAVIOUR EXISTED AND THE DEMAND DID NOT.** Found while answering Joe's question *"should we review the market and its vendor to ensure it is wired and working properly?"* — and his own instinct was the diagnosis: *"the vendor would collect from the farm and keep emptying it."*
  - **⛔ D171 BUILT THE LEG AND NOTHING EVER RAN IT.** `BehaviorSystem.PlanMarketErrand` has had a third leg since then — *clear a workplace buffer that can no longer take a whole armful* — written precisely so `crops-and-orchards.md §3.2`'s *"running it dry is the market's job"* would finally be true. **`LabourQuota.MarketersWanted` counted errands by looping over households and nothing else.** With every family at target the quota was zero, nobody was put on the market, and the leg could not fire however full the farm stood. A trader who happened to be working for some other reason would clear it; that was the only way it ever ran.
  - **⭐ IT IS D36's OWN RULE HELD FOR TWO OF THREE LEG TYPES.** *"Bounded by errands and never by spare hands"* is what keeps `MarketersWanted` from becoming D52's make-work — and the third errand type was added to the *behaviour* without being added to the *demand*. **Two places that must agree, and only one of them told**, which is D148's shape one control over and D142's three call sites.
  - **⭐ THE FIX IS ONE CONDITION WITH TWO CALLERS, NOT TWO COPIES OF ONE COMPARISON.** `SimWorld.BufferWorthClearing` is the single door; pasting the two-line test into the quota beside the one already in the errand planner would have been **making the fix the same way the bug was made**. Guarded by `TheQuotaAndTheErrandAskTheSameQuestion`, which walks a farm's buffer from empty to full and requires the two answers to agree at every level.
  - **⚠️ AND IT BARELY MOVED THE FARM, WHICH IS THE HONEST RESULT AND WORTH RECORDING.** Joe's live complaint is a farmer sowing 5 tiles of 13. Measured after this fix: a farm ten ticks out went **96% → 98% brought in, and 59 → 60 tiles reaped.** **The buffer now gets cleared, which is what D171 intended, and the sowing cap is untouched** — `ReapableShareAt` still scales the field by the granary walk. *A real bug, correctly fixed, that is not the bug Joe reported.* The steading slice is still the one that has to deliver his twelve tiles.
  - **⭐ It also answers the laborer question without building anything.** Joe: *"laborers could also move farm food to the granary if they are idle — it should be the vendor's job before the laborers job though."* The vendor's job is now actually staffed, so the buffer is cleared by the trade the design already assigned it to. `stock-limits-and-laborers.md §5.2`'s **0.0% of ticks** measurement for laborer hauling was taken **before the farm existed** and is stale, but the case it would serve is the one this closes.
  - **663 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 665. One golden moved — the seam**, which is the only village in the suite with a farm in it. Both fifty-year goldens are unmoved, correctly: neither places a farmhouse.
- **D183 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ GIVE, NEVER TAKE — SKILL DECAY IS DELETED, AND A TICK OUT ON THE WORK IS WORTH MORE THAN A TICK WAITING FOR ONE.** Joe, on being shown D182's measurement: *"A villager assigned to a job gains mastery per tick whether or not they are actively engaged. Idle foresters still gain, and so do idle farmers. Active workers gain more than idle ones. **Let's give to the player, not punish or decay.**"*
  - **⛔ `skills-catalog.md §3.4` REQUIRED DECAY, AND ITS PREMISE IS ARITHMETICALLY IMPOSSIBLE.** The argument was *"a fifty-year-old who did six jobs is a master of six"*. **Mastery costs a fixed share of a working life and a life holds at most four even working every waking tick — measured, the most anybody reached in eighty years was one.** A career is still a choice; **the choosing is done by the clock, not by a punishment.** *The spec's fear was unfounded and its cure was the disease* — D182 measured the shipped rate taking **37% of everything one forager earned**.
  - **⭐ A TICK OUT ON THE JOB IS WORTH 1.5 OF A TICK WAITING FOR ONE**, in hundredths so the weighting is a percentage with no float near sim state (D2). **"Out on the job" includes the walk**: a forester who spends nine ticks walking and three felling did twelve ticks of forestry, and counting only the three would charge a distant hut twice for a commute D112 already makes it pay for. **An idle forester still gains**, because they are idle for want of logs and that is not their doing.
  - **⭐ TWO COUNTERS, BECAUSE ONE WOULD HAVE MADE THE PANEL LIE** (Joe's call between the two). `Ticks` is the honest calendar fact the panel and the log quote — *"seventeen years in the woods"* means seventeen years — and `Work` is the weighted total mastery reads. **One counter would have overstated a forager's life by about a fifth**, and had the village announce *"twenty years"* about somebody the panel one click away called seventeen. **So the mastery line quotes the person's own years, not the config's**, which also makes it vary per villager instead of always saying the same number.
  - **⚠️ The measured consequence, stated rather than discovered later:** time out on the job varies by trade — **forestry 88%, woodcutting 82%, foraging 41%, trading 30%, building 27%** — so a forester accrues about **27% faster than a builder.** That is real divergence and it is **the good kind**: the player can see it and act on it, which is §2.3's traceable pressure rather than the invisible tax decay was.
  - **⭐⭐ AND THE DESIGN PROMISE LANDED WITHOUT TUNING A SINGLE NUMBER.** §3.3b wants *"a master for the back half of their life"*. Ages at mastery over eighty years: **34, 35, 37, 37, 38, 39, 39, 40, 42, 46, 49, 49, 49, 55** — median **39**, against a lifespan of 55–79. `mastery_years` stays at twenty; **no per-skill mastery number was needed after all**, and `SkillRow.MasteryYears` stays inert (D182).
  - **⚠️ C# WOULD NOT GIVE THE COMPILE-TIME GUARD THIS WANTED, AND THE FALLBACK IS THE ONE THIS PROJECT ALREADY HAD.** Classifying every `VillagerState` as work-or-waiting begged for an exhaustive switch with no `_` arm, so a new state would be a build failure — **C# still demands the arm for out-of-range casts (CS8524), and adding it silences the missing-name check too.** So it walks the enum in a test, exactly as `DescribeState` already does after falling through to raw enum names for **seven of seventeen states, every one added after it was written**.
  - **660 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 662. The three state-hash goldens moved a second time in one day** — and `ComputeIgnoringSkills` returns the same values it did before *any* of this landed, so both moves were counters and nothing else.
- **D182 · 2026-08-22 · ⛔⛔ DECAY IS WHAT STOPS PEOPLE MASTERING TRADES — AND IT TOOK THREE ATTRIBUTIONS TO FIND, THE FIRST TWO OF WHICH WENT INTO DOCUMENTS.** Joe approved **per-skill mastery numbers** (§12) on the strength of D181's account of *why* a forager takes 32 calendar years to reach 20 years on the task. **Building the measurement to derive those numbers destroyed the reason for having them.**
  - **⛔ WRONG CAUSE #1, AND IT IS IN D181 AND WAS IN THE HANDOFF: *"D44 stands seasonal work down in winter."*** The evidence was **1 of 4 able adults hold a job in mid-winter against 4 of 4 in summer** — a **headcount**, read as **availability**. They are different things. Measured directly, **foraging, forestry, woodcutting and trading are all worked in all four seasons**: somebody is always on it, there are just fewer of them. *A number that is true can still be evidence for the wrong claim.*
  - **⛔ WRONG CAUSE #2, caught before it shipped: derive each trade's mastery from the share of a year it is staffed.** That measures **demand**, which is the player's business. Woodcutting came out at 25% of a year — not because a woodcutter rests nine months but because this village wants one occasionally — so mastery would have been set at **five years**, and a player who staffs three huts properly would mint masters in five. **Overfitting a data file to one village's economy**, and the guard that would have licensed it was one I wrote myself.
  - **⭐⭐ THE ACTUAL CAUSE, TICK BY TICK OVER SIXTY YEARS: Agnes held foraging for 12,240 ticks — MORE than the 9,600 mastery requires — and kept 7,600. Decay took 4,640, which is 37% of everything she earned, and she never became a master.** Mabel, who held trading 70% of her adult life against Agnes's 44%, lost **nothing** and sailed past it. **The variable is not the trade and not the season. It is how continuously one person holds one seat** — and a villager spends over half their adult life off any given trade, because D46's reshuffle moves them every three years.
  - **⛔ SO THE DECAY RATE IS THE TRAP `skills-catalog.md §3.4` EXPLICITLY FORBIDS**, and its own derivation is what hid it: *three years away costs one year* was derived against `labour_reshuffle_years: 3` **on the assumption that three years away is an occasional event.** Measured, it is the normal state of a career. §3.4's words are *"a decay rate that punishes [the reshuffle] would make the labour allocator feel like a trap"* — it does, invisibly, and the player cannot see or control the thing doing it.
  - **⭐ Recommended fix, for Joe: a grace period before decay begins**, at least `labour_reshuffle_years` long, so an ordinary reshuffle cycle costs nothing while a genuine decade away from a trade still does. **You do not forget a trade because the village borrowed you for a summer.** That is a stated fact about the world (D165) and it is derived against the cadence *correctly* this time — against how long people are actually away, rather than against how often the allocator runs.
  - **`SkillRow.MasteryYears` ships inert** — the mechanism Joe approved, with **no row setting a number**, because the measurement removed the reason to set any. Per-skill numbers are a real tool if the trades ever genuinely diverge; **they are not the fix for this**, and shipping them tuned to a cause that does not exist would have buried the one that does.
- **D181 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ PHASE 3 LANDING 1 — PEOPLE NOW GET BETTER AT WHAT THEY KEEP DOING, AND NOTHING READS IT YET.** The proficiency substrate (`specs/skills-catalog.md §11.2.1`): `Villager.Skills` accrues time on the task, is hashed sparsely in id order, and is visible on the panel and in the log. **Six skills are rows in config, not values in an enum** (§4.1, D168) — the second place this project has got that right, after the crop id.
  - **⛔⛔ THE NO-OP GUARD THE SPEC ASKED FOR CANNOT BE WRITTEN, AND THE SPEC IS THE ONE THAT WAS WRONG.** §11.2.1 required landing 1 to be a *"provable no-op: goldens unmoved"*. **The goldens are full state hashes, and proficiency is hashed state that grows from tick one — so they move by construction, and no amount of care makes them not.** The spec reasoned by analogy from `crops-and-orchards.md §4`, where the map golden genuinely held because **the generator never produces the new terrain values**. Proficiency is produced immediately. *The analogy does not carry, and the sentence had been sitting in a Definition of Done for a week.*
  - **⭐⭐ SO THE CLAIM WAS RESTATED IN A VOCABULARY THAT CAN BE TRUE: *nothing anybody DOES changed*.** `StateHash.ComputeIgnoringSkills` fingerprints everything except the counters, and at fifty years it is **byte-identical to both fifty-year goldens and the seam golden as they stood before this slice**. Same positions, same stores, same births, same deaths. **That is a stronger statement than hash equality — it names which half moved** — and it is the same device `PerSiteYieldTests` uses one system over, where a terrain-only fingerprint excludes soil so the guard says more than *"the map changed"*. **§3.2b's own warning arriving from the other direction: a guard that cannot pass is worse than no guard, because it gets "fixed" by being weakened.**
  - **⭐ A TICK COUNTS WHILE SOMEBODY HOLDS THE TRADE, NOT ONLY WHILE MID-ACTION** (§3.6). §3.1's *"the time they have spent doing it"* invites the tight reading — count only `gather_ticks` actually being swung — and **§3.3b's own arithmetic rules it out**: *"a child born in year 1 works at twelve and masters at thirty-two"* is twenty **calendar** years, and nobody is mid-action every tick. It also refuses a feedback loop nobody designed: **landing 2 shortens the action, so under the tight reading a master would accrue more slowly the better they got.**
  - **Decay is derived, not picked** (§12): `labour_reshuffle_years` is 3, so **three years away costs one year of the trade** — the widest rate at which one full reshuffle cycle costs less than it bought, which is what stops the allocator being the trap §3.4 forbids. **The floor is one year**: *"not to zero"*, stated as a plain fact about the world rather than as a high-water mark nobody can read.
  - **⭐⭐ THE MASTERY LINE FIRES, AND IT IS THE FIRST THING IN THIS DESIGN A PLAYER WILL FEEL** (Joe's ask, D174): *"Agnes has farmed these fields for 20 years. There is nothing about this ground left to learn."* Once, on the edge, naming the person — `SkillProgress.Mastered` is what makes *once* true, and it is also §5.4's **record of achievement** arriving early: permanent, dies with the person, **grants nothing**.
  - **⚠️ AND THE PROBE FOUND THE THING §3.6 REFUSED TO GUESS AT: TWENTY YEARS ON THE TASK IS NOT TWENTY YEARS OF CALENDAR, AND THE TRADES DIVERGE BADLY.** Over eighty years of the shipped village: **foraging's best took 32 calendar years to reach 20 on the task, trading 34.** ~~Because D44 unstaffs seasonal work in winter (measured: 1 of 4 able adults hold a job in mid-winter, 4 of 4 in summer).~~ **⛔ THAT ATTRIBUTION IS WRONG AND D182 OVERTURNS IT** — the winter figure is a *headcount*, and it was read as *availability*. Foraging is worked in all four seasons; there are simply fewer people on it. **The measured cause is decay.** See D182. A second measurement fell out of the same probe and stands: **on the exact year edge the reshuffle has torn every allocation down and not yet rebuilt it, so 0 of 4 hold a job for one tick every three years.**
  - **⭐ Nine guards checked red across seven deliberate breaks, and the exercise found a blind one of mine.** `LeavingATradeStopsTheClockOnItThatTick` sampled on a year edge, so *"the number did not move"* was two effects cancelling — no growth, and no decay only because the floor happened to protect a first-year worker. **Removing the floor turned it red for a reason unrelated to what it claims to test.** That is the handoff's *a guard can be green and blind*, caught in the act rather than three sessions later.
  - **660 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 662; three state-hash goldens re-taken in their own commit for one stated reason** (D152). The map golden is unmoved, correctly — the valley is untouched.
- **D180 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ A CONTROL WHOSE ONLY FEEDBACK CONTRADICTED WHAT IT DID — AND THE FEATURE BEHIND IT HAD NO WORDS AT ALL.** Joe, one sitting after D178 merged: *"the ground: toggle… it stays as 'off' regardless. I can't really tell which areas are good or bad."*
  - **⛔ THE OVERLAY WAS WORKING THE WHOLE TIME. `_soilButton.Text` was written inside `CycleDetail` — the *routes* button's handler.** Pressing **Ground** flipped the wash in silence and left the button insisting *"Ground: off"*; pressing **Routes** afterwards would suddenly relabel it correctly. **The one thing the control did was tell the player it had done nothing.** That is D103's *a feature the player cannot reach does not exist*, arriving through the label rather than the layout — and the fix is the rule `CycleDetail`'s own comment already stated: *two places writing that text would eventually disagree.* One writer now, and `g` binds it.
  - **⭐⭐ AND THE SECOND HALF OF HIS SENTENCE WAS A SEPARATE, LARGER MISS: NOTHING IN THE GAME STATED SOIL IN WORDS.** `per-site-yield.md §5` asked for the wash **and** a sentence on the farm's panel; only the wash shipped. **So a wash was the entire channel** — which is why one broken button made the slice invisible rather than merely awkward, and it is the same finding as D86's unpainted work ground: *the sim recorded it, the panel counted it, and nothing on screen changed.* Now any tile the player clicks says what its ground is worth, and a farm says what **its own field** averages — not its doorstep, because soil is regional at lattice 8 and a farm's ground straddles regions. `AFarmReportsTheGroundItWorksNotItsDoorstep` guards exactly the one-line shortcut that would have passed a lazier test.
  - **⭐ THE PROBE KILLED THE HYPOTHESIS IT WAS WRITTEN TO CONFIRM, WHICH IS THE THIRD TIME THIS PROJECT HAS PAID FOR THAT LESSON** (D178's smoothing, D179's slow-test tagging). The plan predicted the wash was faint by construction: `MakeSoilRegional` bilinearly interpolates between lattice draws, so a typical tile blends four of them and should regress toward the middle. **It does not.** Measured over 9,360 dry tiles at seed 12345: **p10 70%, median 101%, p90 135%, min 32%, max 165%.** So *rich* at 115 and *thin* at 85 name **31% / 44% / 25%** of the valley — three words each pointing at ground you can walk to. **A colour retune was on the plan and the measurement deleted it before a line of it was written.**
  - **⚠️ The wording departs from `§5`'s own proposal on purpose.** It suggested *"about a fifth more than average"*; what shipped quotes the share, because the line directly above it in the same panel has said *"62% of what this hut would yield in full woodland"* since D112. **`skills-catalog.md §7`'s `proficiency 73` warning is about a bare number with no referent**, which a share of a stated thing is not. Two conventions in one panel would be the worse failure.
  - **The brush still does not say so once per stroke** (§5's third bullet), and it is named in the spec rather than forgotten. **644 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 646; all four goldens unmoved** — the view and two read-only helpers, no sim state touched.
- **D179 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ THE TEST SUITE WENT FROM NINETEEN MINUTES TO TWO AND A HALF, AND THE CAUSE WAS ONE `for` LOOP.** Joe: *"we have to address these long 'wait for the full run' things… it's been almost an hour and a half."* Then, to a proposal to tag slow tests: ***"measure it first."*** **He was right to insist, because the measurement found something entirely different from what the proposal assumed.**
  - **⛔ THE PLAN WAS WRONG AND THE DATA SAID SO IMMEDIATELY.** The proposal was to tag long-horizon acceptance runs as slow and split the suite. **The suite was already ~9.5× parallel** — 10,720 CPU-seconds finishing in 1,132s wall — so throughput was never the problem, and tagging would have shortened the inner loop while leaving the real cost untouched.
  - **⭐ FOLLOWING THE SLOWEST TEST DOWN.** `Phase0SimTests.LifespanVariance_StaysInTheConfiguredBand` took **560 seconds — nine minutes — to read one integer off fifty villagers.** It never simulates a tick. `VillageNameTests.DifferentSeedsGetDifferentNames` took 204s to read 24 strings. **Between them, 764 CPU-seconds building 74 valleys for two trivial fields** — which pointed at the real question: *why does building a world cost anything at all?*
  - **⭐⭐ AND THE ANSWER WAS NOT WHERE ANYONE WOULD HAVE GUESSED. `MapGenerator.Generate` costs 1 ms. `SimFactory.CreatePhase0` cost 4,069 ms** — the valley was 0.02% of building a world. The remaining four seconds were **41 travel-cost flow fields at ~99 ms each**, built during the founding.
  - **⛔ `TerrainCostField` WAS AN O(n²) DIJKSTRA.** For each of 9,600 tiles it scanned all 9,600 for the cheapest unsettled one — **about 92 million iterations per field.** *This has been in the project since D41 and nothing ever measured it.*
  - **⭐⭐ AND THE FIX IS THAT DIJKSTRA WAS NEVER NEEDED. Every passable tile costs the same to cross, so the cheapest unsettled tile is always simply the next one out of a FIFO queue — with uniform weights, Dijkstra *is* breadth-first search.** Not a priority queue: a plain queue, and one visit per tile. **4,069 ms → 22.4 ms a world, 182×. The suite went 18m52s → 2m30s.**
  - **⭐ THE PROOF THAT IT IS EQUIVALENT WAS ALREADY IN THE SUITE, WHICH IS THE NICEST PART.** Shortest-path distance is a property of the graph rather than of the order it is explored in, and `StepFrom` reads nothing but the finished cost array in a fixed neighbour order. **So if a single tile of a single field had changed, all four goldens and the determinism test would have moved. 641 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped — unmoved.** ⚠️ The old comment about *"ties broken by tile order"* was describing something that could never affect the answer: two tiles at equal cost settle in either order and get the same number either way.
  - **⛔⛔ AND IT CARRIES A TRAP AIMED SQUARELY AT §2.6, WRITTEN DOWN IN THREE PLACES BECAUSE IT WILL NOT ANNOUNCE ITSELF.** Desire-path roads say crossing thresholds *"lowers pathfinding cost, creating a reinforcement loop."* **The day a worn path is cheaper than grass, breadth-first search silently returns wrong answers** — it keeps the first route it finds rather than the cheapest, and nothing throws. **Then, and only then, go back to a priority queue** (`PriorityQueue<int, long>` keyed on `((long)cost << 20) | index`), **never to the scan.** ⚠️ **No guard in the suite would catch it**, because every one of them describes a valley where the uniform-cost rule still holds — the symptom would be villagers taking scenic routes for a phase before anybody noticed.
  - **⚠️ THE PROCESS LESSON IS MINE, AND JOE PAID FOR IT.** I ran the full suite **four times** in one slice, twice to discover things I already knew were going to break. *A run is for a verdict, not for finding out what you could have reasoned out* — and the standing habit of measuring before optimising applies to the tooling as much as to the village.
- **D178 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ PER-SITE YIELD IS SMALLER THAN THE QUEUE SAID AND TWO PROBES EACH KILLED A DESIGN BEFORE IT SHIPPED.** Joe: *"start per site yield."* On `slice/per-site-yield`; spec at `specs/per-site-yield.md`.
  - **⛔ THE AUDIT FIRST, BECAUSE IT RESHAPED THE WHOLE SLICE. §4 called this *"the largest re-derivation on the board"* and over half of D58 had already shipped under other names.** The **7-tile bound stopped being a fence in D120** — `ChooseSite` scores it, and *"a home beyond it is a family the village feeds less well, not one it refuses to house."* **Gathering has had per-site yield since D112**: `GatherYieldAt` is `gather_yield × wooded tiles ÷ ring tiles`, so a hut in thick wood already pays more. **What was missing was the farm, and the half of D58 nobody had built** — *distance costs, **and** distant sites pay better*, without which per-site yield is in D58's own words *"a tax on sprawl that buys nothing."* **Nothing in the sim rewarded distance; `VillageEconomy` does not read travel cost at all.**
  - **⭐⭐ PROBE ONE KILLED THE ALGORITHM THIS SPEC HAD ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN.** The draft said *smooth the per-tile noise into regions*. **Smoothing makes site-to-site variance worse**, because averaging noise regresses everything toward the mean — it destroys amplitude rather than creating structure. Measured across 104 candidate thirteen-tile fields: **p90÷p10 of 134% raw, 123% at two passes, 113% at eight — against 200% for value noise at lattice 8.** *The mechanism intended to raise the number it existed to raise was lowering it, and only a measurement could have said so* (METHODOLOGY §3).
  - **⭐⭐ PROBE TWO KILLED AN INFERENCE THAT LOOKED AIRTIGHT.** `ChooseFoundingSite` runs at step 4 and soil at step 5, so the founding site is chosen with **no knowledge of soil whatsoever** — from which the spec concluded the founding ground was *"already safe-not-rich by construction."* **It is not.** Chosen without knowledge means the percentile is **uniformly random**: across eight seeds the founders landed at the **99th, 93rd, 91st and 83rd** percentile in four of them. **Half of all games would have had the valley's best ground on the doorstep**, deleting the entire reason to go anywhere. ✅ **And the same run showed the payoff is real**: the best ground sits **12–50 tiles out at 177–193** against a median of 99–134, in every seed.
  - **✅ WHAT SHIPPED. Value noise at lattice 8** — sample the already-drawn array on a coarse lattice and interpolate — measured at **182–198% p90÷p10** on the shipped valley. **A cap, not a reduction, near the founding site**: ground within ten tiles is capped at `ReferenceSoil`, so it can only ever take away, never add, and **the founders' fields yield at most exactly `crop_yield_per_tile`.**
  - **⭐ AND THE DRAW ORDER SURVIVED, WHICH IS WHAT LICENSES THE MAP GOLDEN TO MOVE ALONE.** The reshaping consumes **no draws**, so stone, iron, woodland, the river and the founding site are byte-identical. **Proved by A/B against `main` rather than asserted** — the terrain fingerprints, water/forest/stone/iron counts and founding sites match exactly across three seeds, and those fingerprints are pinned as a guard. *Soil was laid down early in D18 precisely so this day would not have to move it; the foresight paid for itself.*
  - **⭐ `crop_yield_per_tile` IS LOCKED AND UNTOUCHED, AND NOW MEANS SOMETHING PRECISE.** Soil is a multiplier around `VillageEconomy.ReferenceSoil` — derived from the generator's own range, never typed — so **average ground yields exactly what it yielded before this existed** and the locked 67 acquires a definition it never had: *the yield on average ground*. **The same device `skills-catalog.md §3.2` uses one system over**: a multiplier that averages to one leaves every derived number standing.
  - **✅ AND THE DISTANCE HALF IS D171'S BUG.** `HarvestOneFarmCanBringIn` asks *this* farm's real haul now, not the average farm's — so a badly-sited farm **commits less ground** rather than committing the same and rotting the difference. ⛔ **`farm_store_cap` untouched**, measured at nought to seven points (D171).
  - **⭐⭐ REDS COUNTED, AND EACH HALF IS CAUGHT BY ITS OWN GUARD ONLY.** Regions off → the spread guard reds **3 of 3** (132–136% against a 150 bar) and the founding guard stays green. Cap off → the founding guard reds **3 of 3** (197, 200, 161 against a reference of 120) and the spread guard stays green. *Neither guard is confused by the other's absence, which is what counting is for.*
  - **✅ WHAT IT MEASURES, BUILT: a farm ten ticks from its store went from 46% brought in to 96%, and still reaps 59 tiles in ten years against a near farm's 144.** *The rot is gone and distance still costs* — both halves of D58, and a fix that made the far farm **equal** to the near one would have deleted the decision the slice exists to create. Site-to-site soil spread is **182–198%** against 134% for per-tile noise. **641 passing, 0 failing, 2 skipped of 643.**
  - **⚠️ AND `AFarmsHarvestFallsOffWithDistanceFromItsStore` HAD TO BE RE-BASED, GOING RED FOR THE BEST POSSIBLE REASON.** It was written this same day to *characterise* the bug, and said in as many words that a bar of *"a distant farm must also bring in 75%"* would be asserting a fix nobody had designed. **The fix now exists**, so it asserts both halves instead. *A guard that outlives the rule it was written for looks exactly like a regression* (D150) — and the honest response is to re-base it, never to relax it.
  - **⚠️ ALL FOUR GOLDENS MOVE, FOR ONE REASON — AND THE SPEC ASKED THE QUESTION IN ADVANCE.** It predicted the two fifty-year goldens would **not** move, since neither village ever places a farmhouse (D162), *"and if they do, that is a finding, not a nuisance."* **They do.** Soil is mixed into the map and the map is in every state hash, so a soil change moves every village hash whether or not anybody farms. **The farm reasoning was right and the map reasoning was missing** — which is D157's rule from the other side: *a golden is silent about what it does not reach, and loud about what it does.*
- **D177 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐ MASTERY BITES IN PHASE 3 AND MILESTONES ARE LOG LINES — two answers that each delete a risk rather than trading one for another.** Joe: *"mastery in phase 3, milestones as log lines."*
  - **✅ MASTERY IS GATED BY NOTHING.** D176 left open whether a tech node should permit it; the answer is no. **A later node may raise the ceiling *further*** — which is the reading of Joe's original *"along with a node of the tech tree"* that survives, the tree **extending** mastery rather than **permitting** it. Reinforced as a guarantee (`skills-catalog.md §6.7`) and as a failure-mode row (`tech-tree.md §11`), because it is the kind of rule a later slice breaks by accident.
  - **⭐ AND IT IS WHAT KEEPS PHASE 3 FROM SHIPPING D56'S SHAPE.** Gating mastery behind Phase 4 would have left Phase 3 shipping proficiency that **accrues, is visible and changes nothing** — *exactly* the clothing system that measured as a no-op over 300 years and was blocked for it. **The anti-vacuity guard becomes writable in the phase that ships the feature**: *a village that never teaches produces measurably less than one that does*, asserting the **effect** rather than the bookkeeping. **A guard that cannot pass yet is worse than no guard, because it gets "fixed" by being weakened.**
  - **⭐ AND THE PHASE NOW LANDS IN THREE PIECES, ONLY THE FIRST OF WHICH IS A NO-OP** (`skills-catalog.md §11`): the **substrate** (hashed, visible, goldens unmoved), then **mastery biting** (moves the goldens), then **the mixed founding and the seeded rhythm together** (moves them again, once). *Landing them apart is what makes a regression attributable* — D157's own lesson that a hash is evidence only about the code it executes.
  - **✅ MILESTONES SHIP AS LOG LINES, WHICH RESOLVES D176'S SEQUENCING CLASH RATHER THAN CHOOSING A SIDE OF IT.** The collections live in the town hall (Phase 4) while §4's queue had promised a milestones **panel** at the end of Phase 3. Log lines in Phase 3, collections home in Phase 4. **And it is the better shape on its own terms:** D161 deferred the panel because *"a panel three-eighths full for two phases reads as an unfinished feature"* — **a log line is never three-eighths full**, so that objection stops applying instead of being overruled. D161's entry is annotated in place rather than rewritten.
- **D176 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ ONE WORD WAS DOING TWO JOBS, AND SPLITTING IT DESIGNS THE TOWN HALL.** Joe: *"Mastery — only from the person who dies… but how does that interplay with the library and knowledge transfer/writing? Maybe from the whole village until they have writing, and then achieved mastery that occurs after having writing/library will put that mastery knowledge into the library and they can't ever lose it (and it is added to the 'collections' tab of the town hall along with all of the other unlockables… hmm I wonder if building the town hall is what unlocks the tech tree?)"*
  - **⭐⭐ THE FINDING IS LEXICAL, AND IT DISSOLVES MOST OF THE QUESTION. "Mastery" has been used for both *a person's years* and *what that person worked out*.** Split into three and the design falls out: **proficiency** (Mabel's twenty-five years — one person, dies with her, never writable), **a technique** (crop rotation, which she worked out *because* of those years — the village's, and exactly what `tech-tree.md`'s `Known`/`Established` states already govern), and **a record of achievement** (*"this village once had a master farmer"* — permanent, and grants nothing). `skills-catalog.md §5.4` is the table; `tech-tree.md §3a` carries it as an annotation.
  - **⭐ AND MOST OF WHAT JOE DESCRIBED WAS THE TECH TREE'S OWN STATE MACHINE, RE-DERIVED INDEPENDENTLY.** *"From the whole village until they have writing"* **is** `Known` → `Established`: a technique held only in living heads dies with the last head, one in a library survives the funeral. **Nothing new was needed for it**, which is the strongest available evidence the shape was right the first time.
  - **✅ WHAT IS LOST WHEN A MASTER DIES: the technique, never the tier** (Joe's call). **Mastery-the-tier is not a node and cannot re-lock** — it is twenty years on the task, and *a village can forget crop rotation but it cannot forget that people get good at things*. **That closes D175's cliff**: there is no state in which somebody works twenty years and is told they may not be a master. Written as a guarantee in `skills-catalog.md §6.7` and as a failure-mode row in `tech-tree.md §11`, because it is the kind of rule a later slice would break by accident.
  - **⭐⭐ THE TOWN HALL GATES THE KNOWLEDGE *SCREEN*, NOT THE TREE — and that distinction is the whole design** (`tech-tree.md §7f`). Gating the tree behind a building the player chooses to raise would convert DOING, CRISIS and SEREN from emergence into a menu unlock, and **would kill §7a's own best argument**: *"the player does not set out to invent writing — they set out not to starve, and writing is what a well-run granary eventually produces."* A granary keeper inventing notation must not be waiting on a civic permit. It would also flatten the early game, which is D161's gap made worse. **So: before a town hall the village log narrates every discovery on its edge; after it, the whole roster is browsable. *Anecdote → archive*** — a real payoff for a civic building instead of a tax on progression. ⚠️ **The at-risk warning is the stated exception and narrates from day one**, because §2.1 requires knowledge-at-risk to be visible and actionable and a warning nobody can see is the funeral surprise this design refuses.
  - **✅ THE MUSEUM IS THE TOWN HALL'S COLLECTIONS TAB**, not a separate building — which is what `buildings-plan.md` already meant by *"records, census, lineage — **not a stats screen**, the place where the village's memory is kept."* Two of Joe's 2026-08-15 notes turned out to be one building.
  - **⛔ AND THE COLLECTIONS GRANT NOTHING, WHICH IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN A SCOPING CHOICE.** This is the one place *"they can't ever lose it"* needed care: applied to **capability**, permanence breaks three of the four guards `tech-tree.md §11` uses against the ratchet that kills the late game — hard shelf capacity, decay and fire. Applied to a **record of what happened**, it breaks nothing at all. **A lost technique still appears in the collections** — *we knew this once* — which is §5's wreckage rule expressed as a sentence rather than a ruined building, and is the best thing they do.
  - **⚠️ LEFT OPEN DELIBERATELY, AND IT IS A SEQUENCING CLASH RATHER THAN A DESIGN ONE.** §4's queue puts **the milestones panel at the end of Phase 3**, while the town hall — which now owns the collections — is a **Phase 4** civic building. The honest options are *milestones ship as log lines in Phase 3 and gain their collections home in Phase 4*, or *the panel moves to Phase 4 entirely*. **Joe's call, and flagged rather than settled quietly**: two documents disagreeing about sequencing is exactly what D159 spent a session unpicking. — ✅ **Settled the same day by D177: milestones ship as log lines in Phase 3 and gain their collections home in Phase 4. There is no panel.**
- **D175 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ THE FOUNDERS ARRIVE AS A MIX OF TIERS, AND EVERY VILLAGER GETS A SEEDED RHYTHM AT BIRTH — which together close D28 at the opening rather than a century later.** Joe: *"I would like a seeded personal rhythm at birth. Maybe the founders could be a mix of masters, mids — whatever that is — and novices? Could be a master woodcutter or gatherer or apprentice forester, know what I mean?"*
  - **⭐⭐ THE MIXED FOUNDING IS THE BEST IDEA IN THE SKILLS DESIGN, AND IT DOES THREE THINGS AT ONCE.** **(1) It makes the four founders *people* in the first frame of a new game** — *"Otto, master woodcutter; Agnes, apprentice forester; Hattie and Wendell, who have never done any of this"* is §1.4 arriving before the player has done anything at all, against four identical units with different names. **(2) It gives the opening a shape to read**: a party with a master woodcutter and nobody who can farm is a *different* opening, which is the argument §2.5 already makes for seeded maps — *a second playthrough is a different place, not the same place played again*. **(3) It finishes the lockstep fix**, because founders at different tiers do different work in different numbers of ticks from day one.
  - **✅ AND THE SEEDED RHYTHM STOPS BEING A STOPGAP.** D28 ranked it second behind personal time-on-task and called it *"treats the symptom rather than the cause"* — **but under D174's floor, skill breaks symmetry only over decades**, so the opening Joe watched at 4× would have stayed synchronised without it. **It is now half the fix rather than a consolation**, with a hard bound: *if the offset changes what anybody produces across a year, it is too big*.
  - **⛔ THE HONEST COST, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE ONE D174 REMOVED: the founding is no longer today's founding, and it gets EASIER.** A party with a master in it starts **above** the floor, so **`cold-start.md`'s five measured ticks will move and the goldens move once.** D174's byte-identical guard survives but **relocates to a posed all-novice village** — the thing that actually tests the floor rule — and **a guard that tries to assert it about the real opening will fail, with the temptation to weaken it rather than pose it properly.** ⚠️ **The unmeasured arm is whether a master gatherer makes the opening trivial**; probe before implementing (METHODOLOGY §3).
  - **⭐ TIERS ARE A READING OF ONE NUMBER, NOT A SECOND STORED THING** — *"master woodcutter" is a sentence and "proficiency 73" is a spreadsheet* (§1.1). Proposed ladder **novice → apprentice → journeyman → master**, where **"journeyman" is the word for Joe's *"mid"***, the one tier with no plain-English name — which is presumably why he wrote *"whatever that is"*. Two sources of truth for one fact is D148's bug and D76's seam.
  - **⚠️ RECOMMENDED: FIXED COMPOSITION, SEEDED TRADES.** Always the same *shape* of party; **which trades they hold comes from the seed.** A seed that hands you four novices and one that hands you two masters are not two playthroughs, they are a good run and a bad one — and **whether you survive should not be decided before you press play** (§0.1: *the challenge is in the planning, never in the punishment*). Joe's call.
  - **⚠️ AND A QUESTION I ASKED BADLY.** *"Can the mastery node re-lock?"* meant nothing to Joe, and rightly — it presumed a reading of his own sentence. `tech-tree.md`'s rule is that a technique known only by living people is **lost when the last of them dies untaught**; if a node *gates* mastery, one badly-timed death could switch mastery off for the whole village including people who spent twenty years earning it. **But *"we introduce mastery along with a node of the tech tree"* reads equally as *ships in the same slice as*, which has no such problem.** §12 now asks the plain version: **should anything be able to take mastery away from the village as a whole, or only from the person who dies?**
- **D174 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ TODAY'S BEHAVIOUR IS THE NOVICE FLOOR, NOT A MID-CAREER REFERENCE — Joe's answer is better than the spec's, and it deletes the risk the spec had named as Phase 3's biggest.** Joe: *"can today's behaviour be the novice floor? i.e. the founders are novices? and we introduce mastery — skill improvement numbers — along with a node of the tech tree?"* And: *"twenty years sounds good, and it should be noted in the event log when someone achieves mastery."*
  - **⭐⭐ THE FLOOR BEATS THE SPREAD, AND THE REASON IS ONE SENTENCE ALREADY IN §2.2.** D173 proposed skill as a *spread* around today's behaviour, reference at a working life's average — which held the economy still but paid for it by making **a village of novices poorer than today's**, so the founding got harder and `cold-start.md` needed re-deriving. **Joe's floor deletes that cost outright**: a novice behaves exactly as villagers behave today, nobody is ever worse, and mastery is headroom above. **And it fits the architecture better than the draft did** — §2.2 already says *`VillageEconomy` derives the **survival floor**; the player sets the ceiling above it*. **The derivation is a floor and the novice is the floor**, so it goes on answering *what must one pair of hands bring back so the village does not die* about the least skilled person in the valley, which is the honest worst case a survival number should be solved against.
  - **⭐ AND IT TURNS A TOLERANCE INTO A HASH.** D173's guard had to assert that a reference village produced *within a stated tolerance* of today's. Under the floor it is stronger and simpler: **a village of novices is byte-identical to today's, and the cold start's five measured ticks do not move.** A no-op to be proved rather than a risk to be survived.
  - **⭐⭐ THE SURPLUS HAS SOMEWHERE TO GO, WHICH IS WHAT STOPS MASTERY INFLATING THE ECONOMY.** Stock limits already stop production at what the player asked for (D62, D128, D141), so mastery cashes out as **the same output from fewer hands**, and the hands it frees become laborers (D63, D66). **That is D161's mid-game answer arriving through the front door** — *"the years the founders become worth learning from"*, with a mechanism under it at last.
  - **✅ MASTERY IS TWENTY YEARS ON THE TASK, AND THE VILLAGE SAYS SO OUT LOUD.** Against a working life of about fifty-five (twelve, D156, to a lifespan of 55–79), so **a founder who sticks to one trade is a master for the back half of their life** — §2.1's own *"a farmer with 20 years in the fields"* meeting the sim's real lifespan numbers rather than a figure picked to fit. **A child born in year 1 masters at thirty-two, so mastery and the first grandchildren arrive together**, which is the generational loop doing the pacing instead of a timer. Content, not derivation (D165's split), so it lives in `data/`. **Narrated on the edge, one line, naming the person** — Joe asked for it by name, and it is the first thing in this whole design the player will *feel*.
  - **⛔⚠️ AND THE FLOOR CREATES THREE THINGS THE SPREAD DID NOT — named now rather than found in Phase 3.** **(1) The founding stays in lockstep**, because founders who all start at the floor start *identical* — and D28 is a symmetry problem, not a variability one. **Skill now breaks it late instead of immediately, and the opening Joe watched at 4× is unchanged**, so D28 is only half discharged and its own stopgap (*a seeded personal rhythm, drawn once at birth*) may be worth taking anyway. **(2) A whole phase of a system with no mechanical effect**, if mastery waits for a Phase 4 node — **and D56's clothing is this project's record of shipping exactly that**. Survivable only because the legibility half is real from day one; **the anti-vacuity guard must be written knowing it, or it will be "fixed" by weakening it.** **(3) Can the mastery node re-lock?** `tech-tree.md §3`'s ordinary rule would take **every master in the village at once** — a cliff, and precisely the *unfair because unforeseeable* failure §2.1 names. It probably wants to be `Established`-only or civic-gated rather than a `PEOPLE` node. **(2) and (3) are Joe's calls and are open in §12.** — ✅ **Both settled the same day. (1) was closed by this entry's own two answers.** **(3) by D176:** mastery-the-tier is not a node at all, so there is no cliff — what re-locks is a *technique*. **(2) by D177:** mastery bites in **Phase 3**, gated by nothing, so the decorative-phase risk never arises.
- **D173 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ `specs/skills-catalog.md` IS WRITTEN, AND ITS LOAD-BEARING DECISION IS THAT SKILL IS A SPREAD RATHER THAN A BONUS.** Joe: *"skills-catalog as docs-only on main."* Queue item 2 done; nothing is built and the spec says so in its status line.
  - **⭐ WHY IT HAD TO COME FIRST, RESTATED FROM THE OTHER SIDE.** `tech-tree.md` is written entirely on top of a proficiency model **that does not exist**: `Villager` has `Vigour` and `VigourStage` and no concept of skill at all, there is no apprenticeship and no teaching. Its anti-ratchet rule — *"a record preserves the method, not the proficiency"* — needs a zero to start near and a *there* to have been. **§6 is now a stated contract** of what Phase 4 may assume, and `tech-tree.md`'s header points back at it, because a promise made in one document and read in another is exactly where D159 found five specs lying.
  - **⭐⭐ THE DECISION THAT MATTERS: THE REFERENCE IS A COMPETENT VILLAGER (§3.2).** Every derived number in this game — `gather_yield`, `firewood_per_split`, `crop_yield_per_tile`, `MaxHomeToWorkTiles`, the fuel budget — is solved against a villager with **no skill concept**, because that is the only villager the sim has ever had. **If skill multiplies output above that baseline, every one of them is wrong the day it ships.** So skill is a *spread*: the reference behaves exactly as villagers behave today, novices are worse, masters are better, and a working life's average sits on the reference. **Losing a master hurts and gaining one helps, without a single derived number moving** — against the alternative, which is *an economy-wide re-derivation wearing a character system's clothes*. **D122 froze nineteen people the last time that chain moved, and it moved by one tile.**
  - **⚠️ AND THE HONEST COST IS WRITTEN DOWN RATHER THAN DISCOVERED: a village of novices is poorer than today's, so the founding gets harder.** Four founders who have never done the work are exactly what `cold-start.md` measures. **It is named as the single biggest risk in Phase 3 and as a probe-before-building item** (METHODOLOGY §3), the way D53's cold model and D56's clothing were both measured as no-ops before a line was written.
  - **⭐ SKILL SCALES DURATION FIRST AND YIELD SECOND, WHICH DISCHARGES D28** — open since 2026-07-26. Today *only* yield is scaled, in six places, all `x * villager.Vigour / 100`; **nothing anywhere scales duration**, which is why two adults of one household holding one job are on the same tile **99.9% of ticks**. Two people who take different numbers of ticks to do the same thing stop arriving together and never re-synchronise. **The 99.9% is on record, so the guard is falsifiable rather than a vibe.**
  - **⭐ SKILLS ARE DATA ROWS, NOT ENUM VALUES — D168's discipline applied at the first opportunity since it was written.** A skill is an id, a name, the work that grows it and whether it can be written down; nothing switches on a skill by name. **Six on day one, one per job that exists**, and ⛔ **none for laborers**, because D66 already ruled a laborer is a position in the priority order rather than a trade — *a skill in being spare is a contradiction*, and it would quietly make the fallback a career.
  - **⚠️ Four things refused by name**, so they are not re-proposed: **no talent or birth-luck** (it would make the most important thing about a person unforeseeable, which is §2.3's failure mode wearing a character sheet); **no skill-gated job refusal** (D120 deleted the last fence in this game and traded it for a consequence — skill may make somebody a better choice, never an ineligible one); **no per-pair apprenticeship screen** (that is a slotting UI on the one axis the whole game refuses it — the lever is a policy); and **no numbers that want a running sim**.
- **D172 · 2026-08-22 · ⭐⭐ PHASE 2 IS MERGED, AND PER-SITE YIELD IS SCHEDULED AT LAST.** Joe: *"merge once it passes, per-site yield behind skills-catalog."*
  - **✅ Phase 2 closed and merged to `main` via [PR #4](https://github.com/joemachen/bclone/pull/4)** — ⚠️ **#4, not #3**, which every document said for a day: number 3 went to the closed screenshot-hook PR that D160 rescued and redid. **A doc that names an artefact by number should be checked against the artefact**, which is D159's lesson in miniature and cost nothing to fix here only because it was caught the same hour.
  - **⭐ PER-SITE YIELD MOVES OUT OF THE UNSEQUENCED LIST AND INTO THE QUEUE AT POSITION 3**, behind `skills-catalog.md` and ahead of Phase 3. It has been *"the biggest payoff on the board"* since D58 and unscheduled ever since, on the honest grounds that it wants a fresh session — and **what changed is that a second system walked into it independently.** D171 measured a farm ten ticks from its store bringing in **46% against 93% next door**, for the same reason gathering has: `FieldTilesOneFarmerKeeps`, like `gather_yield` before it, is **one number for every site in the valley**. *Two systems arriving at the same missing mechanic is the signal that it is a mechanic and not a tuning problem.*
  - **⚠️ Behind the catalogue rather than in front of it, and the reason is the shape of the work.** `skills-catalog.md` is writing; per-site yield is measuring, and it reopens `gather_yield`, `MaxHomeToWorkTiles` and the fuel budget together — D122 froze nineteen people the last time that chain moved. **A re-derivation wants a session with nothing else in it.**
  - **⚠️ And it owes the player a sentence when it lands.** D167 made the rot line mean *you over-painted* or *you lost a farmer*; **distance is a third cause the game cannot yet say**, and a rot line nobody can act on is exactly the weather D167 spent a decision deleting.
- **D171 · 2026-08-22 · ⛔⭐⭐ THE FARM'S SHORTFALL IS DISTANCE, NOT THE BUFFER — and that puts Joe's bug on top of §5's biggest open decision.** Joe, given D170's diagnosis: *"can we add a storage component to the farm itself so farmers have less of a walk? … the vendor can collect the food from the farm's stores (which aren't huge — don't want to eliminate the granary) and move it to the market (or the granary if the market is full)."*
  - **✅ THE MARKET RUNS THE BUFFER DRY AT LAST, WHICH `crops-and-orchards.md §3.2` HAS PROMISED SINCE THE FARM SHIPPED.** Ruling 1 said *"the buffer is free, and running it dry is the market's job"*; ruling 2 built only the market's **sourcing** half, so a trader would take from a farm to fill a hungry larder and never to empty one. A third leg now offers **clearing a workplace buffer**, ranked against every other leg on travel cost through the same `Offer` — so a trader passing the farm clears it and one across the village does not detour, which is ruling 2's own shape.
    - **The condition is derived, not tuned** (D16): a buffer is worth clearing exactly when it **can no longer take a whole armful**, which is precisely when `HaulTheHarvest` stops choosing it. No threshold, no new number.
    - **Where it goes is the existing path on purpose.** A collecting marketer already ends in `HaulingToStore` → *nearest store with room*, which is the market when the market has room and the granary when it does not — Joe's sentence, reached without a second way to find a store (D145).
    - **Checked red and counted: 1 of 1.** The companion is arithmetic and correctly indifferent — and **its first draft asserted something the design contradicts**: *"a farm with room is never touched"* is false, because ruling 2 may still source from a near farm at any fill level. It passed only because one season was too short for that to happen. Re-stated as the condition.
  - **⛔⭐⭐ AND THEN THE MEASUREMENT KILLED THE PREMISE, INCLUDING MINE.** The whole diagnosis rested on *the buffer takes one load, so the walk is long*. Measured across distances, ten years each:

    | farm → granary | brought in | with a 13-armful buffer |
    |---|---|---|
    | next door | 93–96% | — |
    | 6 ticks | 52% | 59% |
    | **10 ticks** | **46%** | 46% |
    | 22 ticks | 25% | 30% |

    **46% at ten ticks is Joe's village to the point.** And raising `farm_store_cap` from one armful to thirteen moves it by **nought to seven points**. **The buffer is not the lever; distance is** — so his locked number stays locked, on evidence rather than on deference, and the clearing errand is worth **+4 points** rather than the fix.
  - **⭐⭐ WHICH MAKES THIS AN INSTANCE OF §5's OLDEST OPEN DECISION, NOT A FARM BUG.** *"The fix is not a bigger number: it is to stop having one global yield. Let yield be a property of the site."* `FieldTilesOneFarmerKeeps` is **one number for every farm in the valley**, so a farm ten ticks out sows what a farm next door could reap, and rots the difference every autumn. **D167 made the rot line mean *you over-painted or you lost a farmer*; distance is a third cause it cannot yet say.** Per-site yield is already §4's queue item — *"the biggest payoff on the board and the largest re-derivation"* — and this is the second system to arrive at it independently, after gathering.
  - **⚠️ AND THE GUARD THAT REPORTS 93% IS NOT WRONG, IT IS BLIND.** `AFarmBringsInMostOfWhatItSows` sites its farm on the first buildable tile beside the founding site, a step from the stores, so the walk the derivation budgets and the walk the farmer takes are the same walk. **Unmoved because it does not cover the case** — D157's finding, third time. `AFarmsHarvestFallsOffWithDistanceFromItsStore` covers it now, and **characterises rather than demands**: a bar of *"a distant farm must also manage 75%"* would assert a fix nobody has designed.
  - ⚠️ **Two wrong turns, both caught by measuring rather than by thinking harder, and both worth recording.** A guard was written claiming the buffer's happy path was untested when `TheHarvestFillsTheFarmsOwnStoreFirst` was ten lines above it — *it is guarded; what is not guarded is the throughput the number exists to create*. And a probe reported a farm reaping 60 of 60 tiles because its *"reaped"* column was counting winter rot as a harvest. **D163's lesson keeps arriving in new costumes: the instrument is as likely to be wrong as the code.**
- **D170 · 2026-08-22 · ⛔⭐⭐ THE FARM'S OWN BUFFER IS WHAT THE DERIVATION ASSUMES AND IT TAKES ONE LOAD A YEAR — so a farmer reaps eight tiles of the thirteen the economy promised.** Joe, on the D169 build: *"the jitter is gone - yay! right column looks good for now. i think the farming is working correctly? the farmer cant harvest as much as it sows, but it seems okay?"* **It is not okay, and his uncertainty was the panel's fault as much as the sim's.**
  - **⛔ THE PANEL WAS CONTRADICTING ITSELF, WHICH IS WHY HE HEDGED.** His screenshot reads *"Staffing farmhouse 1 — 2 of 2"* and, four lines below, *"1 pair of hands can sow 13"*. **Both true and they cannot both be about the same thing:** `Places => StaffingOverride ?? Capacity` is a **ceiling**, while `WorkGroundAllowanceFor` counts `WorkerIds` on purpose (D86 — a hut whose worker dies is overstretched that moment). The farm has two seats and one person, because the village has four adults and three other jobs. **D148's bug, one panel over** — and the professions panel already had the vocabulary, so the staffing row says *"1 working of 2 seats"* now.
  - **⭐ WHAT THE RUN MEASURES, AND THE SOWING CAP IS NOT THE PROBLEM.** One farmer, sowing capped at **exactly 13** every spring — D167 working — and reaping **8, 7 and 3** over three years, with the rot lines (5 and 6 fields) matching to the tile. **46% brought in, against `AFarmBringsInMostOfWhatItSows`, which demands 75% and is green.** *A measurement that disagrees with a guard has found a bug in one of them* (D165, in this very method).
  - **⛔⭐⭐ THE GAP IS THE WALK, AND THE DERIVATION SAYS SO IN ITS OWN REMARKS.** `FieldTileTicks` charges `reap_ticks + radius × travel_ticks_per_unit × 2` — **7 ticks a tile** at radius 2 — and describes it as *"walking each armful back to the steading."* Measured off his trail, the round trips are **10, 10, 11, 12, 14 and 16 ticks**, lengthening through the season. 120 ticks of autumn at ~13 is nine tiles; he got eight. At the budgeted seven it is seventeen, and the field is thirteen. **The derivation and the behaviour describe two different farms.**
  - **⛔ BECAUSE THE BUFFER IS NOT A BUFFER.** In 27 hauls, the farm's own store took **nothing**. And even where it works it can only ever take **one load**: `farm_store_cap` is 100 and `crop_yield_per_tile` is 67, so after one deposit 33 free is less than a whole armful (D165's *"room for the whole load"*, correctly) and every later load walks on to the granary. **Nothing drains it** — the market can, and a four-adult village has no marketer.
  - **⚠️ AND THE GUARD IS SATISFIED BY THAT ONE LOAD.** `TheHarvestFillsTheFarmsOwnStoreFirst` asserts `Store.Food > 0` after an autumn, which one deposit out of thirteen passes. **The happy path is guarded; the *throughput* the number exists to create is not.** D167's shape exactly — *a mechanism can be correct at every step and wrong as a year* — arriving in the step D167 did not look at.
  - **⛔ WHY HIS TOOK ZERO RATHER THAN ONE IS NOT ESTABLISHED, AND IT IS DELIBERATELY LEFT THAT WAY.** Reasoning it out from a log excerpt is what cost D163 and D166 a round each, and this session caught itself doing it twice — once claiming the happy path had no guard when `TheHarvestFillsTheFarmsOwnStoreFirst` was ten lines above, and once reading a probe whose *"reaped"* column was counting winter rot. **So `HaulTheHarvest` writes its reason now** — free space, both costs, which store won — per METHODOLOGY §4's rule that every refusal says why.
